欧州大規模デモが問う財政再建の道 
金融市場が動揺するリスクは当面残存する見通し

○ このところ欧州ではインフレに伴う生活苦や公共サービスの劣化等を背景に各地で大規模抗議活
動が発生している 
○ 大規模デモは緊縮を伴う財政健全化を難しくする要因となる。財政懸念や政情不安は国債利回り
の上昇やユーロ安を招いており、経済への悪影響も懸念される 
○ 来年にかけて、欧州では複数の重要な国政選挙が予定される中、政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金
融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存するだろう

１．欧州で広がる大規模抗議活動 
このところ、欧州では大規模デモが多発している（図表1）。フランスでは、9月以降、教員不足や
学校施設の不備など、教育環境の改善を求める高校生の抗議活動が続いていた。こうした中で、10月
半ばには、物価高への対策や公共サービスの改善を求める市民も加わり、抗議活動は全国規模に拡大
した。 
大規模デモが発生しているのはフランスだけではない。フランスのデモに一部触発される形でベル
図表 1 9 月以降に欧州で発生した主な抗議活動 図表2 EU：生活苦の割合（左）と 
自国が抱える課題（右）

### Page 2

2

ギーやイタリア等でも抗議活動が発生している。また、スペインでは、高齢女性が家賃を支払えずに
強制退去させられたことをきっかけに、住宅不足や住宅価格・家賃高騰等に対する不満が爆発する形
で大規模な抗議活動が発生し、これが遠因となりサンチェス首相は議会の解散に追い込まれた。 
各地で発生する大規模抗議活動の背景には、近年のインフレによる生活苦やこれを受けた政府への
不満がある1。EUにおいて、生活苦を訴える人の割合は低中所得層を中心に2022年以降高まっている
ほか、自国が直面する課題としてインフレや経済状況を挙げる人の割合も高い（図表2）。これらの要
因が、自国政府に対する信頼感の低迷につながっている（図表3）。 
本稿では、生活改善を求めて各国で広がる抗議活動が経済や財政、金融市場に与える影響について
考察する。 
２．大規模抗議活動は財政健全化を阻む要因に 
一連の抗議活動は、2018年にフランスで発生した黄色いベスト運動を想起させる2。当時の参加者は、
自動車に常備が義務付けられている黄色い安全ベストを着用し、燃料税の増税や生活費高騰に抗議し
た。購買力の低下や政府への不満が背景にある点は当時と共通している。異なるのは、政府の追加的
な財政支出の余地が、当時と比べてさらに狭まっていることである。 
欧州債務危機が起きた2010年代前半以降、順調に進捗してきた政府債務の削減は、2020年代に入る
と足踏みしはじめた。パンデミック時の大規模給付やウクライナ戦争後のエネルギー高対策など、危
機対応の財政出動が頻発したためだ。黄色いベスト運動が発生した2018年と比べて政府債務が増加し
たEU加盟国は、27カ国中18カ国にのぼる（図表4）。 
加えて、足元では防衛費増額や社会保障費・利払い負担の拡大など、構造的な財政支出圧力も高ま
っている。各国の次年度予算案を踏まえると、財政赤字は当面、高止まりする公算が大きい（図表5）。
安全保障環境の変化やエネルギー価格の高騰を踏まえ、EUは一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連の追加支
出をEU財政ルールの対象外とする柔軟化措置を設けている。しかし、当該措置を適用しているドイツ
図表3 EU市民の自国政府への信頼度 図表4 EU加盟国の一般政府債務残高の変化 
（2018年～2025年）

### Page 3

3

やベルギーでは、特例対象の追加支出を除いてもなお、財政赤字をGDP比3％に抑制するEUの基準を
超過する見込みである。それでもベルギーでは、連立与党間の予算協議が決裂し、首相が辞意を表明
する事態に陥った。 
フランスを起点に広がった抗議活動は、緊縮を伴う財政健全化の難しさを浮き彫りにしている。2018
年の黄色いベスト運動では、マクロン政権が燃料税増税の撤回や所得支援策の拡充に踏み切ったこと
で、抗議活動が漸く沈静化に向かった。今回は、11月に新たなデモやストライキが計画されるなど、
いまだ抗議活動が収束する見通しは立っていない。各国政府は事態の長期化を回避するため、家計支
援や公共サービス拡充などの対応を迫られる可能性があり、財政拡張圧力が一段と強まることが懸念
される。 
３．政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存 
金融市場は、欧州主要国の財政状況に対する警戒を強めている。 
フランスをはじめとする高債務国では、政治や財政を巡る混乱を嫌気して、10年債利回りが上昇し
ている（図表6）。2010年代の欧州債務危機を受けてECB（欧州中央銀行）は、条件付きで対象国の国
債を無制限に購入可能なOMT（Outright Monetary Transactions）や経済の基礎的条件では正当化されな
い国債市場の混乱が金融政策の波及を阻害する場合に発動可能なTPI （ Transmission Protection 
Instrument）などの市場安定化措置3を整備してきた。当時よりセーフティーネットが整備されている
こともあり、足元の国債利回り上昇が欧州債務危機時のような金融危機につながる可能性は現時点で
は低い。 
とはいえ、金利の上昇は景気下押しや、さらなる財政悪化の要因となる。金利上昇幅が大きいフラ
ンスを例に足元の利回り上昇幅を踏まえて試算すると、設備投資・住宅投資が1年後に3％程度減少す
るとの結果が得られる（図表7）。金利上昇やそれに伴う景気減速は、利払い費の増加や税収の減少を
図表5 主要国の財政収支実績と見通し 図表6 ユーロ圏の高債務国と 
ドイツの10年債利回り

きなかった国。数値は成立前の政府予算案ベース。

### Page 4

4

通じて、財政をさらに悪化させる要因にもなる。 
各国の政情不安や財政への懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営をも左右する可能性がある。足元では、ユ
ーロが主要通貨に対して下落している（図表8）。ユーロ安は、輸入物価の上昇を通じてインフレ率を
押し上げる要因となる。足元のユーロ下落幅を前提に、時系列モデルを用いてユーロ圏のインフレ率
への影響を試算すると、総合インフレ率は0.3％Pt程度、コアインフレ率は0.1％Pt程度押し上げられる
との結果が得られた（図表9）。ユーロ圏では、イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴うエネルギー価格の高騰を背
景に、直近でもインフレ率が加速している。先行きユーロ安圧力が強い状況が続けば、インフレ率に
一段の上昇圧力がかかる可能性がある。しかし、ECBはインフレ圧力が一段と強まっても、状況によ
っては追加利上げに踏み切りにくくなることが想定される。利上げがさらなる長期金利の上昇、国債
市場の不安定化を招くおそれがあるためだ。このように政情不安や財政懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営
を困難にする要因にもなりかねない。 
各国の財政や政治情勢に対する金融市場の懸念は当面残存する可能性が高い。欧州では、今年11月
にスペイン議会選挙、来年にはフランス大統領選挙やイタリア議会選挙など、重要な国政選挙が相次
いで予定されている。大規模デモの発生に加えて選挙も控える中、各国政府は国民に負担を求める緊
縮的な財政政策を例年以上に打ち出しにくくなっている。また、各国の選挙では極右政党やポピュリ
スト政党の勢力拡大が予想され、選挙後の政治はより一層不安定化するリスクがある。このほか、コ
ロナ禍からの経済復興を目的に設立されたEU復興基金の受け取りが終了することも、来年以降の財政
懸念を強める要因になるだろう。同基金の配分が手厚かった南欧や中東欧諸国は、自国の財政負担を
抑えながら公共投資などを通じて経済成長を支えてきたが、来年以降はこうした効果が徐々に剥落す
ることになる。

### Page 5

5

以上のように、2020年代に入ってから、コロナ禍におけるサプライチェーン混乱、ロシアのウクラ
イナ侵攻やイラン情勢の緊迫によるエネルギー高が立て続けに起きてインフレが継続する中、欧州各
国で有権者の生活コスト上昇に対する不満が強まっている。一方、これらの緊急事態に対応するため
財政出動を繰り返してきたことに加え、高齢化に伴う社会保障費の増加、地政学リスクの高まりを背
景とした防衛費増強の必要性、利払い費の増大に直面しており、各国の財政余力は著しく低下してい
る。来年にかけて予定されている主要国の選挙では、極右・ポピュリスト政党の勢力拡大が予想され
ており、政治の混乱に拍車がかかる懸念もある。欧州の政治情勢や財政状況に対する不安が来年にか
けて沈静化するとは考えにくく、国債市場や為替市場が動揺するリスクに引き続き留意する必要があ
ろう。

おかつ実質賃金の伸び率が当該国の長期平均を下回る場合、反政府デモの発生件数が約 7.9％増加すると推計している

### Page 1

日本

2026年 11月 16日 全7頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 7-9月期 GDP（1次速報） 
個人消費の弱さと外需の下押しで、4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長に

経済調査部 チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司 
  エコノミスト 畑中 宏仁

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）と小幅なが
らも 4 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった。設備投資が増加した一方、個人消費が 2
四半期連続で減少し、輸出減と輸入増から外需も成長率を押し下げた。在庫変動を除
く実質最終需要は前期比年率▲2.1％と低迷するなど、内容は悪い。 
◼ 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋0％台前半と小幅ながらもプラス成長に転
じると見込んでいる。個人消費は小幅に増加し、設備投資も増加するだろう。輸出は
米国向けを中心に増加するものの、輸入の伸びが上回り、外需の寄与度は 2四半期連
続のマイナスになるとみている。リスク要因は AI関連需要の下振れと中東情勢の悪化
であり、輸出や個人消費などへの影響に注意が必要だ。

※当社は、11月 20日（金）に「第231回日本経済予測」の発表を予定している。

実質GDPは小幅ながらも 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長 
実質GDP成長率は前期比年率▲0.1％と市場予想を下回り、内容も悪い 
2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は、1次速報値で前期比年率▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）だ
った。小幅ながらも 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となり、市場予想（QUICK調査中央値、前期
比年率＋0.2％）を下回った。なお、12月 8日に公表される 2次速報では 2025年度国民経済計
算年次推計の反映により、幅広い需要項目が改定される可能性がある。 
設備投資が増加に転じ、実質雇用者報酬の増加が続くなど緩やかな景気の拡大基調は続いて
いるとみられるが、在庫変動を除いた実質最終需要は前期比年率▲2.1％であり、個人消費が 2
四半期連続で減少するなど内容は悪い。 
また、交易利得・損失は7-9月期で▲11.4兆円（年率換算額）で1、実質GDPに交易利得・損
失を加えた実質 GDI（国内総所得）成長率は前期比年率▲1.6％だった。原油高などによる交易
条件の悪化で所得が海外に流出し、交易損失は前期から 2.2兆円拡大した。国内の所得環境は
GDPで見る以上に悪化したが、政府のエネルギー高対策が家計や企業への影響を一定程度緩和
した。 
実質 GDPを需要項目別に見ると（図表 2）、民需関連では個人消費・住宅投資が減少した一方、
設備投資は増加した。公需関連では政府消費と公共投資が減少した。公的在庫変動については、
7-9月期に大規模な石油の国家備蓄放出が行われなかったため、実質 GDP成長率への寄与度は
前期比＋0.5％pt と成長率を大きく押し上げた。外需関連では輸出減と輸入増により、純輸出
は実質GDP成長率を押し下げた。 
図表2：実質 GDP成長率と需要項目別の寄与度

（出所）内閣府統計より大和総研作成

1 交易条件（＝輸出デフレーター/輸入デフレーター）の変化による実質所得の増減で、プラスの場合は「交易 
利得」、マイナスの場合は「交易損失」。公表値は 2020暦年連鎖価格のデフレーターから算出されているた 
め（基準年の交易利得・損失額はゼロ）、交易利得・損失額はあくまでも基準年からの交易条件の変化の影響 
である点に留意が必要。

### Page 3

1

3 / 7

GDPデフレーターは前年同期比＋2.4％と 19四半期連続のプラスだった。また、単位労働コ
スト（＝名目雇用者報酬÷実質 GDP）は同＋4.4％と 14四半期連続のプラスで、賃金面からの
物価上昇圧力は足元でも継続している（巻末の関連指標①下段左）。

個人消費は、4-6月期が下方修正された上で 2四半期連続の減少 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と 2四半期連続で減少した。公表前の 4-6月期の個人消費は同＋
0.0％だったが、インバウンド消費額が上方修正されたことで同▲0.3％へと引き下げられた2。
7-9月期は一部耐久財の反動減や、9 月の天候不順といった一時的な下押し要因があったもの
の、所得環境が改善する中で 4-6月期から 2四半期連続で減少したところに、個人消費の弱さ
が見られる。 
国内家計最終消費支出を財・サービス別に見ると、耐久財（前期比▲3.5％）や半耐久財
（同▲1.4％）、非耐久財（同▲0.2％）がいずれも減少した一方、サービス（同＋0.1％）は増
加した（巻末の関連指標①上段左）。内閣府によると、耐久財ではエアコンなどが、半耐久財
では衣服などが、非耐久財ではその他個人ケア用器具（歯ブラシなど）などが押し下げたとい
う。4-6月期には、自動車税の環境性能割廃止や、2027年 4月に予定されているエアコンの省
エネ基準引き上げを見越した買い替え需要が耐久財消費を後押ししたが、その効果がはく落し
た。衣料品などの不振は天候不順が影響したとみられる。一方、サービスでは教育や携帯電話
通信料などが押し上げたという。 
実質雇用者報酬（家計最終消費支出デフレーターで実質化）は前期比＋0.3％と6四半期連続
で増加した（巻末の関連指標①上段右）。労働力調査（総務省）に見る雇用者数は同 0.0％で、
雇用者数で除した1人あたり実質雇用者報酬は同＋0.3％と6四半期連続で増加した（前年同期
比では3四半期連続のプラス）。

設備投資は3四半期ぶりの増加、住宅投資は2四半期連続の減少 
設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と 3四半期ぶりに増加した。建設投資は減少したものの、4-6月期
に特殊要因で減少していた研究開発投資で反動増が表れたほか、機械投資が増加したようだ。
内閣府によると、機械投資はサーバーや半導体製造装置などが増加に寄与したという。 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％と2四半期連続で減少した。原材料費高騰による建設資材価格の上
昇や人件費の上昇が重しとなっており、住宅着工戸数は 6月にかけて増加していたものの、7
月以降は下落に転じている（巻末の関連指標②上段右）。

2 家計最終消費支出は国内消費支出から非居住者家計の国内での直接購入（インバウンド消費）を控除して算
出されているため、インバウンド消費の上方修正は家計最終消費支出の下方修正につながる。

### Page 4

1

4 / 7

公共投資は 2四半期連続の減少、政府消費は6四半期ぶりに減少 
公共投資は前期比▲1.1％と2四半期連続で減少した。資材価格の高騰や人手不足などが重し
になっている上、2025年度補正予算に盛り込まれた公共事業の執行が一巡したとみられる。 
政府消費は前期比▲0.0％と小幅ながらも6四半期ぶりに減少した。内閣府によると、医療・
介護費が増加したものの、中間消費を中心にデフレーターが上昇したことが実質成長率の押し
下げにつながったという。 
また、実質 GDP成長率に対する公的在庫変動の寄与度は、7-9月期は大規模な石油の国家備
蓄放出が行われなかったため前期比＋0.5％ptとなり、成長率を大幅に押し上げた。後述のよ
うに原油の代替調達の進展によって、輸入の増加が実質 GDP成長率を押し下げたが、公的在庫
変動の増加がおおむね相殺した形だ。

輸出減と輸入増により、外需寄与度は前期比▲0.6％pt 
輸出は前期比▲0.2％と4四半期ぶりに減少した。財は増加したが、サービスが減少した。内
閣府によると、財では半導体製造装置や石油製品、基礎化学製品などが増加したという。サー
ビスでは、4-6月期に特殊要因で大幅に増加していた研究開発サービスに加え、インバウンド
や産業財産権等使用料なども減少したようだ。 
輸入は前期比＋2.5％と 2 四半期ぶりに増加した。サービスは減少したものの、財が増加し
た。財では、中東情勢の影響により 4-6月期に減少していた石油・天然ガスが代替調達の進展
によって増加したほか、非鉄金属やサーバー等も増加したようだ。サービスでは、アウトバウ
ンドは増加したものの、内閣府によると著作権等使用料や研究開発サービス、専門・経営コン
サルティングサービスなどが減少したという。 
輸出の減少と輸入の増加により、純輸出（外需）の実質 GDP成長率に対する寄与度は前期比
▲0.6％ptと、4四半期ぶりのマイナスとなった。

10-12月期の実質 GDPは内需と輸出に支えられ、小幅のプラス成長か 
2026年 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋0％台前半と、小幅ながらもプラス成長
に転じると見込んでいる。 
個人消費は小幅な増加に転じるとみている。現金給与総額は前年同月比で 3％を超える伸び
が続いており、10月以降は各地で最低賃金が引き上げられていることから名目賃金の高い伸び
は続くだろう。一方、食料品等を中心に物価上昇率は高まっている。エネルギー価格について
も、資源価格高騰がラグを持って反映されることに加え、補助の一時終了や縮小も物価の押し
上げ要因となり、10-12月期の実質賃金上昇率を押し下げるだろう。設備投資は建設資材価格
と人件費の上昇が建設投資を下押しするものの、省力化投資やAI関連投資を背景に増加基調が

### Page 5

1

5 / 7

続くと見込む。 
輸出は増加に転じるとみている。米国の設備投資はAI関連需要を背景に増加が続くだろう。
米国内での設備投資増加は米国向け輸出の追い風となり、財輸出は増加すると見込んでいる。
一方、インバウンドは中国政府による日本への渡航自粛要請が続く中、増加は緩やかなものと
なるだろう。 
輸入は増加するとみている。原油輸入の回復が続くことに加え、AI関連需要を背景に電算機
類等の輸入も増加するだろう。輸入の増加が輸出の増加を上回ることで、外需の寄与度は 2四
半期連続のマイナスになると見込んでいる。 
10-12月期以降を含めたリスク要因は、AI関連需要の下振れと中東情勢の悪化である。足元
の米国経済では設備投資が景気を下支えしており、とりわけデータセンターや半導体などの IT
関連投資がけん引役となっている。AI関連サービスの世界的な利用拡大に伴い、計算能力の増
強に向けた大型投資が今後も続く可能性は高い。一方で、一部のAI関連企業では収益性や財務
面への懸念が広がっているほか、米中間選挙でAIデータセンター建設の一時停止や規制強化な
どを訴えた民主党急進左派候補者が当選するなど、AI投資を取り巻く環境には変化の兆しも見
られる。ハイパースケーラーが設備投資の拡大ペースを落とせば、半導体や情報通信機器など
の需要を下押しし、関連産業の生産減や輸出減などを通じて実体経済にも波及するだろう3。ま
た、中東情勢が悪化し、原油価格がさらに高騰すれば、物価高を通じて個人消費を一段と下押
しするだろう。 
2027年度までの経済見通しの詳細などについては、11月 20日に発表予定の「第 231回日本
経済予測」を参照されたい。

3 詳細は、畑中宏仁・神田慶司「ハイパースケーラーの投資動向が世界経済に与える影響」（大和総研レポー
ト、2026年11月 12日）を参照。

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

聯強(2347)：資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢
不變 
建議：維持買進

一、聯強為亞太地區最大 3C 通
路商，營收成長以半導體業務最
佳  
 聯強為 3C 電子產品之銷售、配送、維修、量身訂做即時生產之專業通路商，為
亞太地區 3C 通路商龍頭，2026 年前三季產品比重分別為商用加值佔 31%、資
訊消費佔 17% 、 半 導 體 佔 48% 、 手 機 產 品 佔 4% ，成長主要來商用加值
(+112%YoY)和半導體業務(+162%YoY)。聯強海外市場包含中國、香港、紐澳、
泰國、印尼、印度及越南等地，其中以中國、台灣和印尼等區域較佳，營收分別
為 YoY+109%、YoY+78%和 YoY+18%。聯強競爭優勢為透過其四合一經營模
式，搭配其綿密的通路發揮規模經濟，並提供高附加價值的後勤運籌(庫存管理
與快速維為修服務等)，於中國主要競爭對手為神州數碼、天音通信控股。

二、半導體業務成長性佳，3Q26 
EPS 2.93 元創新高並優於預期 
 3Q26 營收 2366 億元(+18%QoQ；+136%YoY)優於預估值 14%，其中商用加
值和半導體營收分別為+116%YOY 和+273%YoY，因秉棄部分利潤不佳專案，
毛利率 3.49%優於前季之 3.4%，營業利益 61.41 億元(+28%%QoQ)優於預估
值 24%，因營運資金需求以及籌資成本上升致使利息費用提高至 9.34 億元
(+106%QoQ)，稅後純益 48.83 億元(+25%QoQ；+90%YoY)，EPS 2.93 元優
於預期之 2.5 元並創新高。

三、資金成本上揚導致業務拓展
較為謹慎，估計 4Q26 EPS 2.34
元 
 由於資金成本上揚致使公司放棄部分利潤不佳半導體業務，十月營收月減 34%，
估計 4Q26 營收 2046 億元(-14%QoQ；YoY+62%)，因產品組合變化轉佳，毛
利率提升至 3.58%，稅後純益 38.99 億元(-20%QoQ；+41%YoY)，EPS 2.34
元。

四、AI 長線需求有助商用加值和
半導體業務成長性 
 集邦 預估 2026 和 2027 年全 球九 大 CSP 資 本支 出分 別為 0.95 兆 美元
(+106%YoY)和 1.53 兆美元(+60%YoY)，且 AI 需求從雲端擴展到企業端，其中
以商用加值和半導體業務受惠幅度最大。 
在商用加值業務方面，在台灣和中國資料中心產品出貨挹注下，3Q26 營收
+116%YoY。展望 2027 年，中國十十五規劃和國產化政策推動政府和國企加速
採用本土品牌，推升聯強代理華為、浪潮等品牌業務成長性，台灣則是接獲數個
半導體大廠 AI 基礎建設專案，加計中小企業 AI 建置需求逐步顯現，商用業務將
為營運成長主要動能之一。 
在半導體業務方面，缺貨和漲價效致使記憶體、SSD 和硬碟業務為半導體業務
中 成 長 性 最 佳 者 ， 且 AI 推 論 亦 增 加 CPU 需求，3Q26 半 導 體 業 務 營 收

YoY+273%，半導體業務為營運成長另一重點業務，但因資金成本上揚，公司將
摒棄部分利潤未達目標專案，此將影響短期營收動能。 
受惠於出貨 ASP 增加，以及原廠擴大促銷力道挽救銷售量，3Q26 資訊家用和
手機業務分別+21%YoY 和+33%YoY。記憶體大漲致使原廠逐步反應成本於 PC
和手機新機售價，並將導致消費者延長換機週期，2027 年此兩塊業務成長性仍
相對保守看待。

五、資金成本上揚將致使接案更
為謹慎，但長期營益率好轉方向
不變 
 聯強今年接獲數個大型 AI 基礎建設專案，致使營收動能以及營運資金需求大增，
3Q26 遇到資金成本上揚，致使利息費用較前季倍增，公司將透過聯貸、可轉債
以及現增降低利息負擔，預期三項籌資工具皆完成後可望降低利息費用 20-
30%。展望 2027 年，儘管營收成長性不如 2026 年亮眼，但接單策略轉趨謹慎
將致使毛利率下滑壓力降低，且預期營業費用增幅有限下，營益率持續好轉方向
不變。

六、維持買進理由  1)預估 2026 和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 9.38 元和 11.07 元。 
2)儘管資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢不變，維持買進
評等，TP 100 元 (2027 PE x 9)。

Document 121: 261112_daiwa_accton

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Accton’s 3Q26 results, released on 12 November.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat/miss expectations. Accton’s 3Q26 
net profit came in at TWD13.1bn (EPS: TWD23.3), in line with our estimate 
(TWD13.0bn) but 6% lower than the Bloomberg consensus (TWD13.9bn).  
Revenue was TWD120.7bn (+26% QoQ, 66% YoY), higher than our/BBG 
estimates by 13%/2%, mainly due to increasing AI card contribution for 
Trainium 3. Nonetheless, gross margin fell by 2.1pp QoQ to 17.6% (vs. our 
19.1% estimate), reflecting rising material costs (especially from memory). 
Consequently, OPM declined by 1.6pp to 13.4% (vs. our 15.1% estimate), 
which was slightly offset by better operating leverage.

Business outlook. We believe gross margin is likely the key highlight of 
the 3Q26 results. Per management, gross margin was diluted due to rising 
memory costs. As such, Accton has shifted its business model from “Buy-&-
Sell” to consignment from September. Since then, we see gross margin 
gradually recovering back to the 20% level. Looking into 4Q26, we believe 
Accton could sustain the gross margin, but the networking chipset shortage 
might weigh on revenue momentum in the short term. The alternative is 
that one of the key switch clients could shift its orders slightly towards 
Spectrum first to ease the near-term shortage issue, in our view. As for the 
AI card business, we believe the total shipment has been largely stable 
since 3Q26. Even when the end-client gradually shifts its product mix from 
air-cooled to the liquid-cooled version, we believe the rising content from 
the liquid-cooled version should be able to offset the slightly lower ASP per 
chipset given there are more chipsets (4x vs. 2x from air-cooled) on an 
ASIC AI motherboard. Looking into 2027, we maintain our original view that 
total AMZN ASIC units will grow c.30% YoY to c.3m as the first growth 
drivers. On top of this, the liquid-cooled version has adopted the PCIE 
scale-up switch, which can expand TAM for Accton, in our view. Lastly, for 
the top-of-rack (ToR) switches, we expect 800G switches to remain the 
mainstream models in 2027 but with rising enterprise usage, which could 
likely be the next growth driver in 2027.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 6-12% to factor in 
higher ToR switch revenue. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating, but lower our 
12M TP to TWD3,000 (prior: TWD3,666), based on a target PER multiple of 
24x (prior: 35x, within its past-3-year range of 10-33x), applied to our 1-
year forward EPS forecast, to reflect the weaker market sentiment. Key 
downside risk: lower-than-expected ASIC server and switch demand.

How we differ: Our 2027E EPS is 5% higher than the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely due to our more positive view on operating leverage.

12 November 2026

3Q26 review: gross margin dilution on memory costs

➢ Fundamentals largely on track with our prior estimates  
➢ Recent pullbacks provide good entry points to build positions 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12M TP to TWD3,000

Company profile 
Founded in 1988, Accton is dedicated to the design and manufacturing of enterprise networking and 
communication products. With its long-term experience and expertise, Accton is one of the leading 
open-source networking ODM partners for major players worldwide, with particular focuses on data 
centres, telco carriers and campus networks. Major products include enterprise switches, metro access 
switches, network applications and wireless products.

### Page 5

5

Accton Technology (2345 TT): 12 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 12 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Accton’s board has 7 directors, with 3 independent directors. The independent directors account
for 43% of all directors. A total of 8 board meetings were held in 2025, and the average actual
attendance rate was 95%.
The backgrounds of independent directors are diversified and their competence includes
business management, accounting, finance, and work experience in the medical industry. We
deem the diversified background as a positive factor for the company’s board quality.
Besides, the Chairman and President are not the same person, which increases the board and
executive management’s quality. Based on its board structure and key executive hiring, we
believe that Accton has already professionalised the family business by allowing external
professionals to run the business for major shareholders.
Capital management 1
Accton declared DPS of TWD15.0 in 2025, representing a 31.8% payout ratio. The ratio was
lower than the 51.2% in 2024 and its historical range of 69-75% from 2018 to 2022. That said, it
still points towards a friendly shareholder dividend policy, in our view.
We attribute the lower payout ratio mainly to the sharp earnings growth and continued capacity
investments in Taiwan and Vietnam amid US-China tensions. The DPS CAGR in the past 10
years was c.23%, which showcased Accton’s capability in capital management. The continuous
improvement in networking capabilities (eg, 400G and 800G switches), alongside expansion into
AI accelerator modules, has helped it differentiate itself from other competitors and strengthen its
position in the white-box switch supply chain for major US cloud service providers.
Related party &
transaction 1
In 2025, revenue from related parties accounted for 0.15% of total revenue, while purchases
from related parties accounted for 2.0% of cost of sales. We see limited risk from related-party
transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
Like most vendors, Accton asks its suppliers to follow: 1) the hazard substance requirements of
the RoHS, Reach and corporate social responsibilities (5 categories, including labour, health and
safety, environmental, ethics and management system) required by the RBA Code of Conduct,
and 2) QC08000 (Hazardous Substances Process Management), while the company conducts
annual reviews to evaluate its suppliers’ performance.
To effectively track, communicate and manage its supply chain to manufacture green products,
each supplier can use the eGreen system® platform, developed independently by Accton, to
learn about environmental protection control and management requirements for components and
materials.
S Data security 2
To ensure information security for customers and the company, Accton obtained ISO/IEC
27001:2013 certification in 2016 and completed the transition to ISO/IEC 27001:2022 in 2024,
with continued verification of its information security policies and controls in 2025. Apart from the
global information security standards, Accton also takes measures to improve its information
security, including:1) Implementing business continuity and disaster recovery mechanisms,
including annual recovery drills for critical systems and databases. In 2025, the company
achieved its target of recovering critical systems within 48 hours. 2) Conducting monthly
vulnerability scans across company-wide servers, network equipment and core systems to
identify potential security risks. Accton also conducts quarterly social engineering drills to
strengthen employee awareness of cybersecurity threats.
In 2025, Accton conducted four phishing simulation exercises involving over 13,000 participant
attendances, achieving an average phishing safe rate of 93%. The company reported no major
information security incidents or personal data breaches during the year.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

Merida (9914.TW)
3Q Briefing Takeaway – New Model Cycle to Drive 2027 Recovery; SBC 
Visibility Remains Limited 
CITI'S TAKE 
We expect Merida’s core business to recover in 2027E, supported by 
accelerating new model launches and largely normalized EU inventories. 
However, we expect the recovery in 2027E to be gradual with US demand 
remaining sluggish. Meanwhile, despite improving pre-tax profits, SBC’s 
profitability remains highly uncertain. We trim our TP to NT$67 factoring in 
a lowered NTM P/E of 11x and earnings cuts. Remain Neutral as we see 
limited near-term catalysts and prefer to await clearer evidence of 
sustainable SBC profitability and stronger core earnings momentum.
Outlook: More new model launches in 2027E to drive a sales comeback — Mgmt. 
attributed softer shipments this year to deliberate reductions in existing models 
ahead of more new product launches in 2027E. Both Merida’s own brand and its 
major OEM customer, SBC, have adopted this strategy, aiming to preserve pricing 
discipline and avoid excessive discounting. Mgmt. expects the inventory 
adjustment to conclude by year-end and most new models are scheduled to begin 
shipping from Jan-27, paving the way for a sales recovery in 2027E.  
EU leads the recovery; China stabilizes while the US remains sluggish — Merida’s 
own brand grew 14% y/y in 9M26 in EU, supported by solid demand for road and 
gravel bikes. The mgmt. believe inventory normalization is largely complete in EU. 
China’s own brand sales grew 1% y/y in 9M26, better than the original expectation 
of DD% decline, helped primarily by entry and mid end bikes. Mgmt. expects another 
single digit sales growth in China in 2027E, supported by resilient commuter 
demand. The recovery of US market, on the other hand, is lagging EU and China amid 
inflationary pressure and persisting macro uncertainties.   
Still low visibility on SBC profitability —The weak bottom-line of SBC was primarily 
due to a conservative effective tax rate assumptions rather than a deterioration of 
its fundamentals. SBC’s 9M26 pre-tax profits grew 42% y/y or +20% y/y excluding 
US tariff refunds, reflecting improving operating performance and cost discipline. 
While improving pre-tax profitability is encouraging, we remain cautious on SBC’s 
near-term earnings contribution until there is a greater clarity on its normalized 
effective tax rate and the sustainability of US profitability. (continued)

Earnings revisions and implications — We lower our earnings estimates by 20%
for 2026E and 6% for 2027E/2028E, mainly reflecting weaker-than-expected core
sales and profitability, alongside continued uncertainty surrounding SBC’s
profitability. We cut our TP to NT$67 based on 11x 4Q26-3Q27E P/E, down from
15.5x, factoring in weaker-than-expected core business and low visibility of SBC.
While we acknowledge the potential for a stronger sales recovery in 2027E as

Merida accelerates its new model launches, we believe the recovery will be gradual
rather than a meaningful acceleration in the underlying market demand.
Meanwhile, SBC’s uncertain net profitability and lingering US market weakness
remain the key overhang.  We would prefer to stay on the sidelines, awaiting clearer
evidence of sustainable SBC net profitability and stronger earnings momentum
from the core business. Reiterate Neutral.

Bull/Bear: Merida (9914.TW)

### Page 5

Merida (9914.TW)
17 November 2026 Citi Research
5
Merida
Company description
Merida is the second-largest bike manufacturer in Taiwan. Founded in 1972,
Merida was initially involved in OEM manufacturing before it started
marketing bikes under its own Merida brand in 1986. Merida has expanded its
footprint globally through its Merida brand and three OEM brands:
Specialized (in which Merida has a 35% stake), Centurion (51% stake), and
Miyata (45%). Merida's own brand contributes around 30-40% of total

revenues. Merida has established a proprietary network of stores throughout
China, with 16 wholly owned subsidiaries and close to 2,000 retailers that sell
only Merida-branded bikes.
Investment strategy
We rate Merida shares as Neutral. Although bike inventories have normalised
in the major markets after aggressive clearance over the past 2 years, slower-
than-expected bike demand recovery continues to weigh on Merida's topline
momentum. The EU market appears to be where we should see better growth

for 2026E, while China market also turns better than expected from 2Q26; yet
with low visibility of SBC profit trend and limited positive catalysts in sight,
we expect the share price to be range bound.
Valuation
Our target price for Merida shares of NT$93 is based on a 4Q26-3Q27E P/E of
11x, a 50% discount to its historical average of 22x during pre-Covid years
(2014-2019), in view of soft order momentum as a result of slow recovery in
bike demand, a volatile margin trend, and low visibility of SBC.

Risks
Downside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade below our target
price include: 1) e-bike adoption slower than expected; 2) weaker restocking
demand; 3) lukewarm sell-through at SBC; and 4) a weaker macro.
Upside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade above our target price
include: 1) e-bike adoption faster than expected; 2) stronger restocking
demand; 3) stronger sell-through at SBC; and 4) a strong macro.
If you are visually impaired and would like to speak to a Citi representative regarding the details

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

that the normalized GM and OPM targets are 25-30% and 13-15% vs. 25.9% and
15.1% in 3Q26.
Management expects shipment of  800 VDC power racks to start from 3Q27 for
its existing hyperscaler customer: It is engaging its existing customers for potential
projects.
Lite-On will expand its capacity in Taiwan, Vietnam, and the US: It aims to lift
non-China capacity to 60% of total by end-2026 and 70% by end-2027.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

◼ 投資建議: 
美系 AI GPU 與客製化 ASIC 持續開案，帶動高階測試座需求。模型預估，穎崴的 NVIDIA 
系統級測試座（SLT）營收將由 2026 年的 33.26 億元增至 2027 年的 83.45 億元，貢獻公
司逾半營收增量。公司目標於 2027 上半年將自製彈簧針月產能提升至 1,400 萬針，有助降
低外購成本，支撐全年毛利率由 40.92% 升至 42.97%。穎崴的矽光子與 CPO 測試方案已涵蓋
晶圓、封裝及模組，若客戶專案進入量產，可望帶來額外營收。 
根據模型，穎崴(6515)目標價 5,711 元。依模型預估的 2027 年 EPS 163.18 元 × 基準本益比 
35 倍估算，較 2026/11/18 收盤價 3,560 元具 60.4% 潛在上漲空間。建議買進 
◼ 未來觀察： 
1. 探針自製率&產能狀況 
2. HPC/AI 業務成長性 
◼ 公司是否涉及經營權爭奪之情事：無。 
◼ 產業概況： 
1. 半導體測試介面市場:系統測試需求高速成長。 
AI GPU、伺服器 CPU 與雲端業者自研 ASIC 擴張，帶動高階晶片封裝後最終測試（FT）與
系統級測試（SLT）需求成長。FT 把關量產電性品質；SLT 模擬實際系統與軟體運作，補足 
FT 對系統整合、長時間與極端負載之測試不足，隨晶片複雜度提升，滲透率持續走高。 
市場預估全球高階 HPC FT/SLT Socket TAM 將自 2026E 9 億美元躍升至 2028E 37 億美元，
CAGR 約 104%（SLT 達約 110%）。動能來自時間與耗材乘數效應：2025–2028E AI 晶片 
FT/SLT 測試時間 CAGR 達 15–20%（如 Rubin SLT 增至 120–150+ 分鐘），壓低單槽產出
並迫使客戶擴增平行測試槽位。隨大封裝、萬級以上高 Pin 數、高頻高速及破千瓦功耗成為
主流，高階測試座須整合訊號完整性、大電流與溫控散熱，帶動 FT Socket 單顆價值量提升 2 
至 3 倍；長時高功耗壓接亦加速探針磨損與更換，創造 4 至 5 倍耗材數量乘數效應。

### Page 2

◼ 公司簡介： 
1. 穎崴主要專注於半導體測試介面之研發設計與製造銷售，目前作為全球最大測試座
（Test Socket）供應商。主要產品涵蓋後端 FT Test、SLT Test、與 Burn-in Test 用之同軸
測試座（Coaxial Socket）、 HyperSocket、Burn-in Socket。今年與 TPI 簽訂五年合作契約
積極擴展前端晶圓測試 CP Test 用之 MEMS Probe Card，並整合主動溫控與液冷技術開
發高工耗測試逤需要的散熱測試解決方案。穎崴目前主要生產基地分布於高雄，分別為
高雄探針廠、高雄仁武廠，仁武廠包含一廠與二廠，其中仁武二廠已於 2Q27 投產。新
竹廠負責探針卡製造。 
2. 1H26 營收結構為 Coaxial Socket 48%、Probe Card 27%、PoP & Plastic Socket 14%、
Contact Element 6%、Others 3%、Burn-in Socket 2%。若按應用別區分，HPC & AI 占比
已達 70%居應用類別首位，其次 Smart Phone 8%、而 Networking／車用及其他合計 
22%。至 3Q26，依 Citi 調研，隨探針卡專案出貨階段性收尾及高階測試座放量，FT 
Test、SLT Test & Burn-in Socket 合計比重回升至 72%。其中 Coaxial Socket 預估 60%、
RF & Plastic Socket 大概 11%、Burn-in Socket 則佔 1%。至於 Probe Card 預估降至 20% 
3. 公司整體合併毛利率於 1Q26 為 43.0%、2Q26 因 MEMS 探針卡占比拉升及新產能折舊影
響降至 38.3%，3Q26 隨測試座比重提高毛利率已回升至 40.5%。各產品線方面，依摩
根大通調研，Socket 與 Contact Elements 毛利率穩定維持在 40%+。而 MEMS Probe Card 
因須外購關鍵探針元件，因此即便 2026 年生效之二期協議已改善獲利，毛利率仍僅約 
30–35%。目前穎崴 Pogo pin 自製程度高達 60%，隨自製彈簧針 pogo pin 月產能由 1H26
的 600 萬 pins 擴至 1H27 的 1,400 萬 pins，將有助進一步支撐 Socket 毛利率。 
4. 客戶比重:截至 2026 年活躍客戶超過 200 家，但營收高度集中於高階 AI、HPC、GPU、
ASIC 與伺服器 CPU 客戶。前十大客戶合計占營收比重達 90%，且 7 奈米及以下先進

### Page 3

製程產品占比高達 89%。根據摩根大通，2026 全年穎崴核心終端客戶營收占比以
Nvidia 居首，佔比大約 50–60%。其次為 AMD，預計 10–15%與 Google 約 2%。
◼ 成長動能：
1. AI GPU／ASIC 推升 SLT 與高階 FT 測試座需求)
AI 晶片朝大封裝、高 Pin 數、高功耗與高速傳輸升級，拉長測試時間並放大 SLT Test 導入
需求，使整體市場進入倍數擴張期。在 TAM 動能上，全球高階 HPC FT Test／SLT Test 測試
座市場規模預計自2026E 的 9 億美元躍升至2028E 的 37 億美元，市場預估從 2026 到 2028
相關產品的市場CAGR 將逾 100%，其中價值量更高的 SLT Test 測試座將由 7 億美元跳升至
31 億美元。隨穎崴切入 Nvidia SLT Test 供應鏈，目前市占率預估由 2026E 的 20% 升至
2028E 的30%，外加 2027 年雲端業者如 Google、AMD 的自研 ASIC 與伺服器 CPU 擴大量產，

FT Test／SLT Test 測試座占穎崴總營收比重預估將於 2027E–2028E 維持於 64%–79% 區
間。根據自有模型估算，測試座業務於 2027E 預計貢獻營收約新台幣 161 億–192 億元、
2028E 擴增至約 274 億–362 億元，整體測試座 2026–2028E 營收 CAGR 區間約為 61%–
89%。
2. HyperSocket 與液冷熱管理產品放量
下一代 AI 晶片正由約 10000 Pin 邁向20000 Pin 以上，且由於 AMD MI455、TPUv9 系列新
世代平台封裝尺寸將跨過 100×100mm 至 120×120mm 門檻，基板翹曲與大電流散熱成為測試瓶
頸。穎崴專利 HyperSocket 結合主動液冷熱管理方案可克服大尺寸封裝翹曲並維持穩定電
性，因此Pogo Pin 單針 ASP 相較既有高階同軸測試座享有約 30%溢價，帶動產品組合持續升
級，目前穎崴公司預估 HyperSocket 與液冷測試解決方案將於 2027 占總營收 5%，但考量客

戶大封裝世代切換節奏，普遍預期放量重心將落在 2028 年。依自有模型的預估，該測試解決
方案預計於2027 貢獻總營收約 3%–5%（約新台幣 9 億–15 億元），2028 有機會放大至約
5%–6%（約新台幣 21 億–32 億元），相較 2027 年呈現倍數級年增動能，並有助高階測試座
在擴產自製化下將 2027E–2028E 合併毛利率支撐於 41.1%–46.2% 區間。

### Page 4

3. 新增產能釋放與 CPO 測試
因應既有產能滿載，高雄仁武二廠已於2Q27 投產，其中新增 Socket 產能自 3 月開出並在第
二季順利認列營收，使 5,000-pin 的等效規格 Socket 月產能自 2026 下半年的 4.2k 套擴增逾
70%至 1H27 的 7.2k 套，自製 Pogo Pin 月產能亦由 900 萬 pins 增逾 50%至 1,400 萬 pins，預
估 2027 年底可達單月 1,600–1,800 萬 pins。新產能開出讓既有高階 AI 積壓的訂單順利轉
化為營收，支撐目前市場與自有模型所預估的穎崴 2027E 總營收區間達新台幣 213.5 億–
303.7 億元（YoY +45%–85%）、2028E 達 351.0 億–528.3 億元（YoY +64%–76%），對應
2026–2028E 總營收 CAGR 區間為 54%–81%（EPS CAGR 區間則達 76%–108%）。CPO 的測試
方案預計2H27 開始小量出貨，營收貢獻大約低個位數以下，至 2028 年進入量產成熟階段，

預計可以貢獻2028 年營收最高至接近 10%。
4. 1H26
1H26 合併營收 65.03 億元，HoH +61.0%、YoY +70.3%（1Q26 為 29.80 億元、2Q26 為
35.23 億元），整體毛利率為 40.5%（1Q26 為 43.0%、2Q26 為 38.3%）。稅後淨利 13.71 億
元（YoY +67.6%、HoH +60.4%；1Q26 為 6.99 億元、2Q26 為 6.72 億元），1H26 累計 EPS
達 38.24 元（1Q26 為 19.54 元、2Q26 為 18.70 元），上半年營收與獲利均創歷年同期新
高。截至最新已公告進度，累計前 9 月合併營收達 110.59 億元（年增 96.7%），同創歷年
同期新高。在產品組合與毛利率比較上，1H26 產品營收比重仍以 Coaxial Socket（48%）與
Probe Card（27%）為核心。
⚫ 與1H25（毛利率 48.9%）比較：1H26 毛利率表現明顯較差（YoY -8.4 個百分點），主

因1H25 銷售較多高毛利的 Non-MEMS Probe Card 及傳統高毛利組合，而 1H26 向
Technoprobe 外購關鍵 MEMS 探針元件的 MEMS Probe Card 營收占比擴大，上述外購元
件結構拉低 MEMS Probe Card 毛利率，使其落在 30%–35%，低於測試座及接觸元件的
40% 以上水準，對整體毛利率形成結構性稀釋。

### Page 5

⚫ 2H26E 穎崴合併營收 79.56 億元（HoH +22.3%、YoY +97.0%），毛利率 41.29%（HoH 
+0.82 ppt、YoY -0.58 ppt），歸屬母公司稅後淨利 17.71 億元（HoH +29.2%、YoY 
+107.1%），基本 EPS 48.76 元（HoH +27.5%、YoY +103.5%）。

⚫ 2026E 穎崴合併營收 144.60 億元（YoY +84.0%），毛利率 40.92%（YoY -4.34 
ppt），歸屬母公司稅後淨利 31.42 億元（YoY +87.8%），基本 EPS 87.04 元（YoY 
+85.5%）。 
5. 2026/2027 
產能與資本支出方面，仁武二廠與下一座新廠預計於 2Q27–1Q28 陸續投產，推升 2027 年探
針月產能達 1,400 萬–1,800 萬支、自製率提高至 50%–60%，5,000-pin 等效測試座月產能於 
1H27 達 7.2k 套等於穎崴整體測試座產能將在 2027 倍增，2027–2028 年度預估的資本支出介
於新台幣 20.0 億–24.0 億元，其中自有模型預估 2027 為 20.0 億到 22.0 億元之間、2028 預估
為 22.0 億到 24.0 億元之間。新產品線方面，受惠高階 Functional Burn-in 升級，2027–2028E 
Burn-in 測試座營收預估為新台幣 6.40 億–10.53 億元，2027 模型預估約 6.40 億元、2028 則
有望到 10.53 億元，占總營收約 3%成為未來關注的成長動能之一。CPO 與矽光子測試預計自 
2H27 起小量出貨，2028E 營收貢獻區間為 5%-10%不等的年營收，端看實際量產與交貨情況。。

⚫ 2027E 穎崴合併營收 238.42 億元（YoY +64.9%），毛利率 42.97%（YoY +2.05 ppt），
歸屬母公司稅後淨利 59.53 億元（YoY +89.5%），基本 EPS 163.18 元（ YoY +87.5%）。

⚫ 2028E 穎崴合併營收 407.33 億元（YoY +70.8%），毛利率 44.33%（YoY +1.36 ppt），
歸屬母公司稅後淨利 111.08 億元（YoY +86.6%），基本 EPS 304.52 元（ YoY +86.6%）。

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 Review: 바닥을 잡는다면 지금  
믹스 개선에 따른 이익률 개선 조금씩 관찰 중 
3Q26 연결 영업이익은 2,612억원(+90.6% YoY)을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,455억원)
를 소폭 상회했다. 밥캣(1,674억원)이 컨센서스(1,761억원)를 소폭 하회한 가운데, 
에너빌리티 부문 영업이익(+179% YoY, OPM 6.6%)이 호실적을 이끌었다. 환율 하
락에도 불구, 가스터빈, 원전 등 고수익성 프로젝트의 비중 상승이 주요 원인이다. 
3Q26 누적 수주는 10.0조원(+85.5% YoY)을 기록했다. 4Q26 가스복합 EPC 수주 
등을 감안하면 연간 가이던스(13.3조원) 달성은 무난하다. 한수원과의 협상 결렬로 
이목이 집중된 체코 두코바니 시공 수주는 2027년 인식될 확률이 높아 보인다. 주간
사가 바뀌더라도 이미 프로젝트에 참여 중인 동사를 배제하기는 쉽지 않다.

대미투자 이후, 연말~연초 수주 모멘텀에 집중 
연말 연초에 다음과 수주 모멘텀이 집중되면서, 주가 상승을 이끌 전망이다. 
1) 대형원전: 정부 자금 중심의 Westinghouse LLI(장납기 기자재) 발주가 몰린다. 
우선 미국 DOE(에너지부)가 제공한 대출을 기반으로 미국 수주가 기대된다.  폴란드 
정부 역시 최근 LLI 발주 작업을 시작했다. 한편, 한국 정부는 연말까지 100억 달러 
규모의 대미 투자 송금을 협의 중인데, 이 역시 DOC(상무부) 주도의 대형 원전 건설
을 위한 LLI 발주에 쓰일 가능성이 높다 
2) SMR: 오래 기다려왔던 Nuscale 향 수주가 시작되고 있다. 우선 29일 모듈 1기 
제작 계약을 체결했다. 현금이 부족한 Nuscale이 용기를 냈다는 건, 곧 Nuscale-
TVA 간 PPA 가 임박했음을 의미한다. 본격적인 수주는 PPA 직후 예상된다.  
3) 가스 터빈: 2026년 초 해외 수주(7기)는 2029년까지 인도 계획이다. 이후 2030
년 슬랏 예약을 완료하고 2031년 물량을 논의 중으로 보인다. 지금은 2030년 슬랏
에 대한 PO가 나와 수주잔고에 인식할 타이밍이다. 역시 수출 물량으로 추측한다.

성장에 대한 기대가 사라진 밸류에이션, 지금이 바닥 
투자의견 Buy를 유지, 할인율 상승을 반영하여 적정주가를 109,000원으로 -17% 
하향 조정한다. 금리 상승, 환율 하락, 트럼프 지지율 하락 등 Top-down 리스크에 
최근 조정을 받았지만, 추가적인 주가 하락 폭은 제한적일 전망이다. EV/선행 수주잔
고 지표는 현재 1.0배 수준까지 하락하며, 원전의 성장성이 주목받지 못했던 
2021~2024년 수준에 근접하게 내려왔다.  
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 30

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 109,000원 
현재주가 (10.29) 56,700원 
상승여력 92.2%

Document 255: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh 규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

Document 305: Chipping in_ how chips have become a huge driver of the dollar (2026-11-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chipping in_ how chips have become a huge driver of the dollar (2026-11-16).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Chipping in: how chips have  
become a huge driver of the dollar

1 
Authors 
Mallika Sachdeva 
Strategist

November 16, 2026 
What do chips have to do with a new era of dollar dominance? As we discuss in 
this paper – a great deal. Chips are not only critical to the future of AI but have 
recently emerged as a huge driver of global trade and capital flows. 
It is well known that the US is dominant in upstream chip technology and 
downstream compute. This note delves deeper into the workings of the middle of 
the chain where flows have been reinforcing the dollar’s global role. The 
manufacturing of chips largely takes place in Asia where chip-related trade 
surpluses have ballooned. As we show, these surpluses are finding their way 
back to the US via investments in US capital markets. 
Asian chip surpluses are not being recycled into US Treasuries like petrodollar 
surpluses were, but into US equities. Foreign inflows into US equities have 
crossed USD1tn a year and become the mainstay of US deficit funding. The 
biggest source of increase over the past year has come from Asia. 
Equity flows have not just been supporting the US dollar but have provided 
financing to the companies driving the AI buildout and chips boom itself. This 
introduces forms of circularity and potential instability to global flows. Moreover, 
equities are a much riskier asset class than bonds – US stocks are three times as 
volatile as Treasuries, with tech stocks up to ten times as much. Growing dollar 
dependence on inherently riskier capital should spill into higher US dollar 
volatility over time. 
The rise of chips as a driver of dollar dominance can be seen as a symptom of 
change in the ultimate source of US power - from geopolitical to technological 
leadership. This power is increasingly concentrated in the private sector with 
dollar supportive flows intermediated via private balance sheets. 
Understanding this new framework and its risks is even more important given 
rapid shifts in news flow around AI, from the US political climate, high-stakes 
capital market raisings, potential tightening in export controls, and the highly 
consequential technology race with China.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

The rise of the chip dollar 
For the first time in modern history, global trade in semiconductors now 
exceeds trade in oil and gas (Figure 1) based on the four quarters of global 
exports till Q2-2026. More relevant from a currency perspective, is that chip 
surpluses in Asia ex-China have caught up to petroleum-related surpluses in the 
Gulf (Figure 2).  Why does this matter and what does this reflect?

Figure 1:  Global trade in chips has overtaken trade in 
oil & gas for the first time in history

Figure 2:  Asia (ex-China)’s chip surpluses are now as 
large as the Gulf’s petroleum-related surpluses

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, ITC Trade Map; Note: Oil & Gas are HS Codes 2709, 
2710, 2711.; Semiconductors is HS 8542

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, ITC Trade Map; Note: Oil & Gas are HS 2709, 2710, 
2711.; Semiconductors is HS 8542; Asia ex-China is Japan, Korea, Taiwan & ASEAN

We argue that the petrodollar is – rather quickly - being superseded by the chip-
dollar. This is both literal in terms of global surplus accumulation, but also 
archetypal. In other words, the petrodollar and chip-dollar are archetypes for two 
different states of the world, and for alternative foundations of dollar dominance.  
The petrodollar regime can be understood as one where US geopolitical 
leadership – over global security, international rules, and free trade – motivated 
global savings to be reinvested in the US sovereign via Treasury holdings. This 
cheapened funding for the US government which in turn supported the financing 
of US public goods provision.  
The emerging chip-dollar regime is one where US technology leadership is 
motivating global savings to be reinvested in US companies – providing funding 
for the US corporate sector to make enormous investments in new technology 
such as AI.  
The transition from the petrodollar to the chip-dollar is therefore a 
manifestation of change in the US’ leadership style - from geopolitical to 
technology leadership. This is not only changing the nature of global imbalances 
and capital flow recycling but is reshaping the foundations of dollar dominance.  
Revisiting the petrodollar regime  
After the 1971 Nixon shock, when the dollar’s convertibility to gold was 
terminated, we moved into a fully fiat currency world. The US dollar since then 
has been backed only by the fiscal credibility of the US government and the 
willingness of the world to finance US deficits by buying US assets.  
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 3

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

The 1974 petrodollar deal provided a crucial pillar of support for the dollar in its 
fiat infancy and sowed the seeds for a post-Bretton Woods regime in which US 
geopolitical leadership supported dollar dominance.  In 1974, in exchange for US 
security and military guarantees, Saudi Arabia agreed to price and invoice oil sales 
in USD. These USD denominated oil surpluses were then reinvested in US 
Treasuries. This was soon replicated across the Gulf. The 1990s and 2000s saw 
huge growth in commodity and manufacturing surpluses around the world. These 
were arguably supported by the US’s global backstop for trade and security – with 
surpluses saved in USD and recycled into US Treasuries via public balance sheets, 
in what can be considered a petrodollar-plus regime.  
We wrote earlier in the year how the Iran war could undermine the critical 
foundations of the petrodollar system by challenging the US security umbrella 
and shifting perceptions around US geopolitical leadership. We maintain that 
these changes are underway. But what has become apparent since then is that 
something else has been rising to take the petrodollar’s place in support of the 
dollar.  
The chip dollar is working differently  
While petrodollar surpluses were recycled into US Treasuries, chips surpluses 
are being recycled very differently.  
We focus on the chip surpluses being built in Asia ex-China. We note that China 
runs a very large semiconductor deficit, despite having the world’s largest overall 
trade surplus - something we will return to later. Asia’s chip surpluses are thus 
primarily hosted in Korea and Taiwan which are large semiconductor 
manufacturers, to a lesser extent in Japan which produces key materials, and 
ASEAN-10 which plays a role in assembly, packaging and testing.  
To explore how chip dollar surpluses are being recycled, we zoom in on South 
Korea – the epitome of the boom in chips trade.  
Korea’s semiconductor exports have grown over 200% YoY this year, with Korean 
chipmakers dominating in the manufacture of memory chips critical to AI 
acceleration. As Figure 3 shows, Korea’s semiconductor trade surplus has 
ballooned to close to USD250bn with 100% of Korea’s overall trade surplus now 
on account of chips alone.  
What is striking is that Korea’s chip surplus is being recycled abroad entirely via 
equities. Foreign sales of Korean equities have accounted for half the capital 
outflows in the past year, while Korean purchases of overseas equities account 
for the other half (Figure 3). Korean investments in overseas equities have been 
five times more than Korean purchases of overseas bonds in the last two years. In 
short, chip surpluses are being saved in global equities, not debt. 
The primary destination of Korea’s overseas equity purchases has been the US. 
While the US accounts for around 50% of global equity market capitalization, we 
note it is 70% of Korea’s overseas equity holdings (IMF CPIS). The US bias is even 
more exaggerated for Korean retail investors – a group empowered by access to 
financial technology. Korean households have 90% of their equity exposure in the 
US (Figure 4). It appears that North Asian capital is flowing back to the very US 
technology companies that are driving the chip surpluses themselves.

16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 4

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

Figure 3:  Asian chip surpluses are being recycled into 
US equities – not US Treasuries like petrodollars were

Figure 4: 70% of Korea’s equity exposure abroad is to 
the US, and that figure is more than 90% for households

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, CEIC; Note: The semiconductor trade balance here is 
measured using Korea’s Customs Service classification for Semiconductors and 
equipment for semiconductor manufacturing

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics; Note: Data based on IMF’s 
Coordinated Portfolio Investment Survey as of end-2025

The reach for US equities by Asian retail is not just happening in Korea. In Japan, 
there have been significant allocations to US focused ETFs, encouraged by the 
NISA scheme. And as we have argued in our earlier paper, the US’s embrace of 
asset tokenization and the upcoming shift by US exchanges to 23/5 trading could 
further embolden and enable retail investors to access US equities. We could thus 
just be at the beginning of this new trend. Countries in North Asia like Korea and 
Japan not only have large savings pools, but also open capital accounts.  
This shift has shown up dramatically on the US capital account. Foreign 
purchases of US equities have exceeded USD1tn over the past year (Figure 5). 
Interestingly, most of the increase in purchases has come from Asia (Figure 6). 
Notably, foreign purchases of US equities are now more than double US Treasury 
allocations, which appear in relative decline. Even foreign purchases of US 
corporate bonds now exceed US Treasuries. This is notable considering the ramp 
up in hyper scaler issuance.

Figure 5: Foreign flows into US stocks now exceed 
flows into US Treasuries by a significant margin

Figure 6: Most of the increase in US equity purchases 
over the past year has come from Asia

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics (TICS data)

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics (TICS data)

16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

Chips are the oil of the new economy  
The power of having oil priced and paid for in dollars was not just the recycling 
of petrodollar surpluses, but the value chain impact of having a key upstream 
resource dollarized. Oil is a critical input to almost every industrial process via 
petrochemicals – it is found in everything from plastic to paints, tires to 
cosmetics. It also plays a role in moving traded goods around the world as the 
transport fuel for ships. We would argue that having oil priced in dollars played a 
role in dollarizing global trade as companies looked to match the currency of 
downstream receivables to the upstream costs of oil. This is consistent with the 
Dominant Currency Paradigm (Gopinath et al, 2020).  
Chips arguably play a similar role in the digital and consumer economy as oil has 
played in the industrial economy. Chips are inputs to everything from consumer 
electronics, to automobiles, computers, communication devices, and most 
recently the bedrock of modern AI systems.  
Indeed, chips make up the lion’s share of spending in the AI buildout. Analysis by 
Epoch AI found that servers represented about 60% of the annualized cost of an 
indicative 1GW data centre in the US (Figure 7). According to the Semiconductor 
Industry Association (SIA), chips represent 95% of an AI server rack's value. In 
simple terms therefore, we can say chips make up over 50% of total capital 
expenditures for an AI data centre. Bloomberg Intelligence estimates hyper-
scaler capital expenditure commitments of close to USD8tn out to 2031 (Figure 
8), which would suggest a further USD4tn of chips demand from AI alone.

Figure 7: More than half the cost of the AI data centre 
build out is going towards chips

Figure 8:  Capital expenditure in the AI buildout may 
still have a long way to go

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Epoch AI; * Note: Based on a stylized model of a US 
hyperscaler AI data center that has 1 GW capacity. CapEx is converted to annual costs 
over each asset’s lifespan, assuming 5 years for IT equipment like chips.

Source: Deutsche Bank Research

To be sure, the market will remain sensitive to shifts in news flow and risks that 
the AI data centre buildout might slow whether due to the changing political 
climate in the US, calls for growing public oversight and safety monitoring of the 
industry, or a renewed focus on export controls.  
There are also open questions as to whether the boom in chips demand will be 
“one-off” as capacity is built or will be ongoing like with oil. Oil is a huge source of 
ongoing dollar flows primarily because of how consistent and trade-intensive it 
16 November 2026
 FX Special Report

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank AG Page 6

is: 100 million barrels are consumed daily with 70% of them being traded. We note 
that the replacement rate of chips in an average data centre is estimated at 3-7 
years, which suggests there could be ongoing demand even once the projected 
capex buildout reaches its zenith. But it remains to be seen whether the chips 
dollar can generate the type of enduring and stable recycling that the petrodollar 
has generated. 
Staying with the comparison between the petrodollar and chip-dollar, arguably it 
was US military presence in the world’s largest oil producing region in the 1970s 
– the Gulf - that gave the US control over the pricing of oil. What is it that gives 
the US control over the pricing and trade in chips? 
US control over chips: technology, economic rents, and supply chains   
The integrated circuit was invented in the US in 1958 with the microchip industry 
flourishing in California to become the well-known Silicon Valley. It is hard to 
overstate the continued dominance of the US in chips since then. Work by Sean 
Kenji Starrs of Kings College London maps the present-day dominance in 
compelling terms1. 
While most chip manufacturing happens in Asia – which is where the trade 
surpluses discussed above are being accumulated – most of the economic rents 
to chip-making accrues to US companies. Around 50% of the value in the 
semiconductor supply chain is in the design process where US companies control 
87% of the revenue (Starrs). The US has a near monopoly on the electronic design 
automation (EDA) software necessary for design, and controls around 60% of the 
revenue in semiconductor manufacturing equipment without which chips cannot 
be made. Materials, manufacturing, packaging and testing of chips happen 
outside the US, but these are worth only around 30% of the estimated value of 
the industry. Indeed, Starrs finds that 70% of the profits from the semiconductor 
industry flow to US firms (Figure 9).  
Furthermore, we note that all key countries involved in the supply chain of 
advanced chips remain US aligned. Japan plays an important role in materials, 
while Taiwan and Korea dominate in production (foundries). Importantly each of 
these North Asian players leans geopolitically towards the US over China. The US 
has arguably been less vocal about withdrawing economic support for security in 
North Asia than with NATO. One can thus posit the existence of a US-Asia chip-
dollar bloc. We note that the Netherlands has a monopoly on EUV lithography 
machines required to make the most advanced chips, but production of these 
machines in turn have a dependency on a US optical equipment firm helping 
grant the US crucial leverage even here.  
The potent combination of US control over key upstream technologies and 
intellectual property, alongside a supply chain located in US allies has given the 
US enormous control over chips. In recent years, the US has actively exercised 
this power via export controls to limit China’s access to US advanced chips and 
technologies. This has been the subject of books from Chip War by Chris Miller to 
Chokepoints by Edward Fishman. The importance of ensuring the integrity of US 
export controls was made apparent in recent comments by US Trade 
Representative Greer who noted that the US wants the “crown jewels” of the 
semiconductor industry to remain in the hands of the US or its allies. The US

1 Starrs, Sean Kenji. 2025. “Ringfencing Globalization: US Structural Power, Techno-Nationalism & Chinese Dependent 
Integration.” In Chapter 8 in Globalization in a Turbulent Era 
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 7

Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

government has arguably had significant influence over the private sector sale of 
US chips technology and may double down on this after recent events at Nvidia 
to limit both direct access to US chips and the leasing of global data centers built 
with US chips to non-US allies (CNBC).

Figure 9: The US is very dominant in chips – controlling 
key upstream technology and an enormous part of the 
market value of the industry

Figure 10: China continues to run a large trade deficit in 
semiconductors – notable given China’s over USD2tn 
manufacturing goods surplus

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Starrs, Sean Kenji. 2025. “Ringfencing Globalization: 
US Structural Power, Techno-Nationalism & Chinese Dependent Integration.” In 
Chapter 8 in Globalization in a Turbulent Er; * Data for 2024 based on 40 chip 
companies in the Forbes 2000 ^ Data for 2000 based on 29 companies

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, ITC Trade Map (HS 8542)

Where does China fit in with all this?  
This note has looked at key changes in the industrial drivers of global imbalances 
(from fossil fuels to chips) and capital flows to the US (from debt to equities). 
Where does China – with the world’s biggest trade surplus – fit in?  
China now runs an over USD2 trillion manufactured goods surplus per year but 
continues to run a persistent semiconductor deficit (Figure 10). While US export 
controls limit access to the most advanced computing chips, China continues to 
import large volumes of memory and logic chips. Restrictions on access to 
advanced manufacturing equipment, particularly EUV lithography, have 
constrained China’s advances in domestic chip-making at the technological 
frontier, although greater self-sufficiency remains a major strategic priority. We 
note that the other drag on China’s trade position is commodities, mostly fossil 
fuels. Notably therefore, the remaining holes in China’s trade surplus are 
dominated by chip-dollar and petrodollar imports. In a forthcoming paper, we will 
look at China’s gains in renewable energy, new energy exports, and electrification, 
examining how these could emerge as long-term sources of power in both the AI 
race, and in increasing China’s independence from the dollar.  
The recycling of China’s manufactured goods surplus remains the topic of 
considerable debate. China’s surplus is in many ways the mirror of the US deficit, 
albeit with growing contribution from Europe, and increasingly intermediated 
through the Global South. There remains a school of thought that these Chinese 
savings find their way to the US through state banks and custodian balance 
sheets. But we note important points of difference with the chip dollar recycling 
we have discussed.  Nearly 50% of the net international assets held by Korea, 
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 8

Deutsche Bank AG Page 8

Japan and Taiwan are in portfolio investments – namely equities and bonds. For 
China, this number is less than 20%. This is consistent with China’s capital account 
restrictions on private capital, as well as a growing geopolitical preference to limit 
exposure to US capital markets. Instead, China holds a much greater share of 
direct and other assets – reflecting growth in Chinese state-directed lending and 
acquisition of hard assets particularly in the Global South. On public reserve 
balance sheets, we note a growing preference for gold over dollar assets. Finally, 
we note a shift towards conversion of China’s surpluses, showing up in a stronger 
RMB and lower domestic rates.  
Bringing it together 
In the age of AI, it is well known that the US is dominant in both upstream chip 
technology as well as downstream computing. The US designs and controls 
supply chains for the world’s most advanced chips and is home to 10 times as 
many data centers as any other country (Stanford HAI). Both are sources of US 
power. This note has delved deeper into the workings of the middle of the chain 
where new flows are reinforcing the dollar’s global role in trade and capital flows. 
The manufacturing of high-end chips takes place in Asia ex-China and generates 
large trade surpluses there. This money is finding its way back to the US via 
Asian investments in US equities and corporate bonds. These capital flows are 
both funding the US trade deficit and providing financing to US companies that 
are driving the AI boom. Chips are thus ensuring the continued dominance of 
the dollar in global trade, payments, capital markets, and private sector savings, 
as well as supporting the US AI buildout seen as crucial to the future.  
There is an element of circularity to be conscious of: equity flows and financing 
for US AI related stocks are being funded out of Asian chip-related surpluses 
which are themselves being boosted by demand from US AI companies. This 
could introduce instability in global flows should demand be reassessed. For 
instance, if consumer demand for compute does not keep up with costly 
investments in supply, this would not just reduce earnings estimates for AI 
companies, but ultimately chip demand. One can see how this would challenge 
foreign equity financing for the US from both a push and pull perspective.  
Separately, as we noted in our last paper, proliferation of Chinese open-source 
models could also undermine the profitability of US companies and the 
attractiveness of US markets to global capital.  
The fact that the US dollar has become more dependent on foreign equity 
inflows than US Treasuries should make it inherently more volatile and exposed. 
Equities by nature are a riskier asset class than bonds. Recent realized volatility in 
S&P500 has been 3 times as much as in a US 10Y Treasuries. NASDAQ volatility is 
double that of the S&P and the Philly Semiconductor Index more than triple.  That 
US deficit financing is now dependent on capital flows into a more volatile asset 
class should spill over into currency volatility in time.  
In sum, US dollar dominance is now dependent on US technology leadership 
driven by private companies, and the flows underpinning the dollar are 
increasingly being intermediated on private sector balance sheets. A dollar driven 
by chips, AI and private capital connections should be inherently riskier.  
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
November 16-20
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 14 Nov 2026

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week’s inflation data ran hot. October’s headline CPI rose 0.4% m/m,
while the core CPI rose 0.3% for a third straight month. The PPI for final
demand rose 0.3% m/m in October after a revised 1.4% in September,
lifting the y/y rate to 6.5%, the highest since November 2022.
This week’s calendar is busy, with the FOMC minutes, Nvidia’s earnings,
Anthropic’s IPO, retail sales, and industrial production. Here’s more:
(1) FOMC minutes & FedSpeak. Minutes of the October 27-28 FOMC

meeting (Wed) should show how many of the 19 participants (12 voters
and 7 nonvoters) leaned toward a rate hike. The Committee held the
federal funds rate (FFR) target range at 3.75%-4.00% in a 10-2 vote, with
Fed Governor Lisa Cook and Cleveland Fed President Beth Hammack
dissenting in favor of a 25bps increase.
Six Fed officials speak this week (Warsh, Williams, Musalem, Hammack,
Goolsbee, and Paulson). Fed Chair Kevin Warsh appears Monday at the

Fed’s AI and productivity task force hearing in the San Francisco Bay
Area. New York Fed President John Williams, who speaks Monday and
Wednesday, said on Thursday that “inflation is far too high” and that
further tightening will be needed. We think the Fed should and will hike by
50bps in December.
FFR futures imply 3.6 rate hikes of 25 bps over the next 12 months
including 2.6 over the next six months (chart). The odds of a 50bps hike at
the December meeting are just 11.5% currently.

### Page 4

The 2-year Treasury bond yield is at 4.86%, well above the 4.00% top of
the FFR target range, with 12-month futures at 4.78% (chart).
(2) Nvidia earnings & Anthropic IPO. Nvidia reports Q3 earnings on
Tuesday, its first report since Jensen Huang’s resignation as CEO.
Revenue is expected to be $109.1 billion, up from $57.0 billion a year
earlier and slightly above the company’s $108 billion guidance. The
stock’s forward P/E is down to 11.5 (chart). The conference call with

interim CEO Colette Kress, who remains CFO, is likely to be more
revealing than the numbers.

### Page 5

Anthropic shares are expected to begin trading Thursday, with a price
range of $70-$78. That implies a fully diluted valuation of $1.26 trillion to
$1.40 trillion. We feel confident about the company’s prospects based on
its S-1 filing.
(3) Retail sales. October retail sales (Tue) follow September’s 0.8% m/m
gain, with sales ex autos up 0.9%. Retail sales ex food services rose 6.2%
y/y in September, and Redbook same-store sales continue to run well

ahead of the official measure (chart).
(4) Manufacturing. November’s NY and Philly Fed surveys (Mon, Thu)
follow solid October readings. Their average edged up to 23.2 in October
from 22.7 in September. The average has run well ahead of
manufacturing production growth this year, which was just 1.4% y/y in

### Page 6

September (chart). Historically, the two have tracked more closely,
suggesting that factory output could accelerate in the months ahead.
October’s industrial production (Tue) follows September’s 0.7% m/m gain,
which lifted the index to 104.0. Capacity utilization was 76.9%.
Manufacturing aggregate weekly hours slipped to 84.0 in October from
84.2 in September, suggesting that factory output weakened last month
(chart).
(5) ADP & unemployment claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading

(Tue) will update a four-week average that has been rising for the past two
weeks (chart).

### Page 7

Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 200,000, up from 189,000,
with the four-week average at 195,250 (chart). Continuing claims fell to
1,697,000 for the week ended October 30, with the four-week average
steady at 1,709,000.

Document 328: Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02)

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports
Europe’s auto deal with China is a good start. But containing China’s export boom
will require a much broader response.
European Commissioner for Trade and Economic Security Maros Sefcovic speaks at a press briefing at the EU Delegation to
China in Beijing, China October 9, 2026. Florence Lo/Reuters
PUBLISHED
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
November 2, 2026 11:02 a.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The year-over-year increase in Chinese automobile
exports to all markets is currently about 3.5 million
cars.
Over ten years, that is 35 million cars. The global
market excluding China is only around 60 million
cars. Japan raised its auto exports by about 3.5 million
cars in the 1970s—but that was over a decade, not
one year. And right now the U.S. and Indian markets

(around 20 million cars) are effectively closed to
Chinese production. The 2026 jump in exports is an
incredible.
China’s domestic market, though, is down 20 percent
this year—that works out to a decline of about 5
million cars. The Wall Street Journal estimates that
domestic sales will fall to around 19 million cars this
year, less than a third of the production capacity of
China’s auto industry.
So even though China’s exports are increasing at an

unprecedented pace, China’s domestic auto sector
isn’t really booming; production is essentially flat, and
intense domestic competition is destroying the
profitability of firms that don’t export.*

### Page 3

3
Europe’s new auto deal with China should be
understood in this context.
The deal limits China’s exports of hybrids (including
plug-in hybrids) to Europe to around 350,000 cars.
That is above the 300,000 hybrids that China
exported to Europe in 2025, but well below the (gulp)
800,000 annualized pace for hybrids in the last three
months of (European) data. Total imports,
annualized, were running at an incredible 2 million a
year pace thanks to strong battery electric imports in

September.

### Page 4

4
China appears to have concluded that it prefers a
guaranteed market for a limited number of cars (and
more or less guaranteed profits) to the risk of a
prohibitive tariff that would cut off all its exports.
And Europe preferred a negotiated deal to the risk of
retaliation and a slowdown in licensing rare earth and
magnet exports.
The Commission also thinks of this as a bit of a
model—more on that later.
I certainly understand the logic of the deal.

Something needed to be done. China’s export of
hybrids to Europe were up an insane 200 percent
year-over-year in the last 3 months of data, which led
to a 75 percent increase for all cars. One more year of
75 percent growth would bring China’s exports to

### Page 5

5
around 3 million cars; in two years, close to 6 million,
and in three years, 10 million—or most of the market.
But Europe still is a long way from a coherent trade
and industrial policy with respect to autos, let alone a
coherent economic policy towards overall trade with
China.
After this deal, as I understand it:
Hybrid imports are capped at 350,000, down
from a projected 800,000 in 2026—and then
rising to 370,000 and 390,000 in subsequent
years.

Battery electric vehicles face a tariff from 25
percent (Telsa, 10 percent base + 15 percent) to
45 percent (10 percent base plus 35 percent).
BYD’s total tariff is at 27 percent, which isn’t
prohibitive. Even with these tariffs, battery
electric imports will total around 600,000 this
year, up from 500,000 last year.
Diesel and gasoline (ICE) cars face a tariff of
10 percent. Europe is importing around
200,000 of these a year (including a significant

number from Chinese-owned MG).
There are no special tariffs on imported parts or
imported batteries, which matters for the growing
number of Chinese factories in Europe. So long as
tariffs are low and the renminbi is undervalued,
Chinese firms still have an incentive to maximize the

### Page 6

6
Chinese content of their “transplant” factories (ask
Thailand).
There is currently no unified European policy on
inward investment that requires joint ventures or
technology transfer either, only a lot of wishful
thinking.**
Sum it up, and it is a market where Chinese exports
are constrained by a hodge-podge of different trade
defense tools, and there is no standard policy for
ensuring that Chinese assembly operations use
European parts rather than import Chinese kits. The

existing operations, which are just starting to scale up,
don’t draw on a European supply chain. They likely
preserve a few assembly jobs at the expense of the
broader automotive ecosystem.
A coherent European (or national) industrial policy
for the automotive and battery sectors is thus still a
work in progress.
The safeguard on hybrids and the resulting tariff rate
quota only stops the bleeding.
And make no mistake, Europe’s position in the global

automotive sector is slipping. Europe still runs an
overall surplus in auto trade, but that surplus is falling
fast if trade in batteries is added to automotive trade
(there isn’t a split between batteries used for energy

### Page 7

7
storage and batteries used by cars in the EU data
right now, at least not that I have found).
Europe’s broader trade policy to China is similarly a
work in progress.
The surge in imports of hybrid cars was particularly
pronounced, but it is by no means the only such surge.
The balance in industrial machinery has eroded
significantly. Consider the following chart, which
shows imports and exports of machinery, electronics
and vehicles (HS 84, 85, and 87) by weight.

### Page 8

8
The balance in chemicals, measured by weight to
abstract from price changes, shows the same trend.
Europe doesn’t have an across-the-board tool for
applying tariffs against China like the U.S. section
301 statute.

### Page 9

9
But that isn’t necessarily an immediate constraint, as
there are plenty of solid safeguard cases thanks to the
current surge in European imports from China.
But if each safeguard needs to include a negotiated
tariff-rate quota that gives China enough guaranteed
market access (and Chinese firms enough guaranteed
profits) to ensure trade peace, Europe needs to get
moving with negotiation in other sectors.
Chemicals and machinery are more diverse sectors

than cars and thus aren’t as amenable to the same
high-level Š ef č ovi č -Wang negotiations the led to the
most recent deal.
This critique isn’t meant as criticism. Europe needed
to act on cars. Look at a chart of automotive trade in
kilos.

### Page 10

10
It’s just that it also needs to act in other sectors: auto
parts, batteries (though that is complicated),
chemicals, and some categories of industrial
machinery. The scale of China’s export boom and the
fact that in most sectors there are no limits to China’s
ability to scale up is a real problem.***
And Europe cannot wait for China to conclude that
it would rather grow on the basis of internal, rather
than external, demand.
I disagree with the IMF’s former top brass on this. A

China that grows on the back of a 1.5 pp net export
contribution for another couple of years will
deindustrialize much of the world. China’s trading
partners cannot step aside and do nothing until
China figures out how to escape deflation using its
own demand. Xi appears more than happy to
continue with his current policy mix and gamble that

### Page 11

11
the world will continue to absorb China’s ever
growing export surplus.
Pascal Lamy, Nicolas Köhler-Suzuki, and Victor Luiz
do Prado of the Jacques Delors Institute recognize
the need for a more systemic response—as do Jean-
Pierre Landau and Sébastien Jean, who have done an
excellent job explaining how China’s micro- and
macro-industrial policies fit together into a coherent,
self-reinforcing whole.
The proposals in the Lamy et al. paper overlap heavily

with the Commission’s current approach to China.
The authors envision a two-pronged approach: first,
negotiating with China to seek agreement on tariff-
rate quotas that effectively limit the EU’s imports
from China; and second, developing a broad yet
targeted tariff-based instrument to address structural
imbalances should negotiations fail. The latter would
give Europe a credible threat should China refuse to
agree to the proposed safeguards.
My own view: don’t let the perfect be the enemy of

the good.
The EU has shied away from developing its own 301
tool out of concern about preserving a “rules-based
system.” I still think a European 301 would allow a
more fulsome response, but understand that
European isn’t as willing as the United States to use

### Page 12

12
tools that at least appear to be outside those
authorized by the WTO. In reality the EU no longer
really wants to trade with China on the same (MFN)
terms applied to other WTO members. That mirrors
the current view of the United States. But the EU is
willing to go through more procedural hoops to get to
a similar result.****
But I also recognize that, absent policy adjustment in
China—notably a stronger currency that allows for
fair market competition—the scope of safeguards and

managed trade will progressively expand.
* One sign: Stellantis is resuming Jeep production in
China, but primarily for export rather than the
domestic market.
** There is a lot of (in my view) somewhat confused
analysis of Chinese investment that works off the
analogy provided by either foreign investment in
China’s auto sector or Japanese investment in the U.S.
and Europe in the 1980s. These are relevant examples
of course, but asserting that Chinese investment will

result in a similar outcome when the underlying
economics differ is a big logical jump. Japanese firms
had incentives to localize their parts production in the
United States from the then-strong yen as well as
from the risk of protection. Foreign firms operating in
China had to do JVs until recently and had incentives
to localize from China’s significant tariff on auto

### Page 13

13
parts and China’s lower cost structure (in part because
of a weak RMB). Tesla’s Shanghai incentives and tax
deal included strong localization incentives, and there
were additional incentives from Tesla’s desire to
qualify for the lists the governed access to China’s
consumer EV subsidies. That is why something like
90 percent of Tesla parts, including its batteries, are
now made in China. Chinese investors in Europe
have no similar incentives to localize right now, yet

many assume that the results will be the same. That,
to me, is naïve. Since the underlying economics differ
(Europe’s cost basis is much higher than existing
production in China) getting Chinese firms to do
more than final assembly in Europe will take stronger
policy tools than were required to get foreign firms to
localize parts production in China. Europe’s
Industrial Accelerator Act would be a step in the
right direction, but more is likely needed. Thailand

provides a useful cautionary example.
*** China can make over 60 million cars, according to
recent reporting from the Wall Street Journal. The
domestic market this year will be under 20 million
cars (down from something like 24 million cars last
year). Even with exports of 10 million, there is ~30
million cars worth of unused auto production
capacity (roughly double European demand). And
China is still investing at a pace that is consistent
with adding close to 5 million of EV production

capacity a year. Chinese firms, who don’t make any
money on domestic sales, are strongly incentivized to

export. The world has never encountered such elastic

**** The more different the legal basis for the

harmonized policy isn’t even a U.S. goal.

earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 30, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.4%. 
 Of the 299 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 81.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 13.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 12.2%. 
 69.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 19.0. 
 During the week of Nov. 2, 144 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings. 
SECTION A: EARNINGS OUTLOOK
